\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

for all
r
and
x
, from which our result follows as a consequence of
Lemma
3.5
. Note that

\displaystyle\mult^{H}\circ(\sigma^{H}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\nat^{(H\phi)^{*}}_{r}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)

\displaystyle=\mult^{H}\circ(\sigma^{H}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\eta\cdot H\phi_{r})\circ\gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mult^{H}\circ(H\eta\cdot H\phi_{r})\circ(\unit^{H}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle=H(\eta\cdot\phi_{r})\circ\mult^{H}\circ(\unit^{H}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle=H(\eta\cdot\phi_{r})\circ H\lambda^{-1}\circ\rho

\displaystyle H\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{r}\circ\mult^{H}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{H})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)

\displaystyle=H\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{r}\circ\mult^{H}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot H\eta)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{H})

\displaystyle=H\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{r}\circ H(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\mult^{H}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{H})

\displaystyle=H(\eta\cdot\phi_{r})\circ H\gamma^{-1}\circ\mult^{H}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{H})

\displaystyle=H(\eta\cdot\phi_{r})\circ H\lambda^{-1}\circ\rho

so (
3.4
) holds by mate correspondence.
∎

This invertibility condition is satisfied, for instance, by Barr extensions of
monads on
\mathsf{Set}
; see
[
24
, 1.10.2(2)]
, and by strong functors.

4.
Double categories as pseudo-algebras

This section is devoted to proving the following result:

Proposition 4.1
.

We have an equivalence of double categories
\mathsf{PsDbCat}\simeq\PsfcAlg
,
where
\mathfrak{F}=(\mathfrak{F},m,e)
is the free internal category 2-monad on
\mathsf{Grph}(\mathsf{Cat})
, and
\PsfcAlg
is the sub-double category of
\LaxfcAlg
consisting of the pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras.

The proof is laid out as follows:

–

We recall the definition of
\mathfrak{F}
, verifying it is a 2-monad.

–

We provide a construction of a pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra from a given
pseudodouble category.

–

We provide a construction of (op)lax morphisms of pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras
from given (op)lax functors of pseudodouble categories. Moreover, we
verify this construction defines a functor
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}\to\PsfcAlg_{\mathsf{lax}}
(and dually,
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{opl}}\to\PsfcAlg_{\mathsf{opl}}
).

–

We prove the aforementioned functor is fully faithful and essentially
surjective.

–

Let
H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
and
K\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{D}
be lax functors, and let
F\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{C}

and
G\colon\mathbb{B}\to\mathbb{D}
be oplax functors, and consider
the induced lax and oplax
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra morphisms (as in
(
4.3
)). Given a 2-cell
\omega\colon GH\to KF
of
internal
\mathsf{Cat}
-graphs, we prove that
\omega
is a generalized
vertical transformation if and only if
\omega
is a generalized
2-cell of pseudo
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras.

We begin by recalling that
\mathsf{Grph}(\mathsf{Cat})
is the functor 2-category
[\cdot_{1}\rightrightarrows\cdot_{0},\mathsf{Cat}]
, whose 2-cells
\theta\colon F\to G
are pairs of natural transformations
\theta_{i}\colon F_{i}\to G_{i}
for
i=0,\,1
such that
d_{j}\cdot\theta_{1}=\theta_{0}\cdot d_{j}
for
j=0,\,1
.

Since
\mathsf{Cat}
is an extensive category with pullbacks, we can define the
free internal category monad
\mathfrak{F}=(\mathfrak{F},m,e)
on the underlying category
of
\mathsf{Grph}(\mathsf{Cat})
.

To extend
\mathfrak{F}
to a 2-monad, let
\theta\colon F\to G
be a 2-cell in

\mathsf{Grph}(\mathsf{Cat})
. We define
\mathfrak{F}\theta
by letting
(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{0}=\theta_{0}
and
(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{1}\colon(\mathfrak{F}F)_{1}\to(\mathfrak{F}G)_{1}
is given at
a composable string of horizontal arrows
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}
by

(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}=(\theta_{r_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{r_{n}}),

which is a horizontally composable string of 2-cells, and
(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{()}=\theta_{0}
.

We must check
(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{1}
is natural; indeed, if
\phi_{i}\colon r_{i}\to s_{i}
is a horizontally composable string of 2-cells, then
\theta_{s_{i}}\circ F\phi_{i}=G\phi_{i}\circ\theta_{r_{i}}
for all
i
, so

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}}\circ(\mathfrak{F}F)(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n})

\displaystyle=(\theta_{s_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{s_{n}})\circ(F\phi_{1},\ldots,F\phi_{n})

\displaystyle=(G\phi_{1},\ldots,G\phi_{n})\circ(\theta_{r_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{F}G)(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n})\circ(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}},

and since
\theta_{0}
is already natural, there is nothing to check for

n=0
.

Finally, note that
d_{1}\cdot(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}=d_{1}(\theta_{r_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{r_{n}})=d_{1}(\theta_{r_{1}})=\theta_{d_{1}r_{1}}=\theta_{d_{1}(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}
, and likewise
d_{0}\cdot(\mathfrak{F}\theta)_{1}=\theta_{0}\cdot d_{0}
.

To verify
\mathfrak{F}
is a 2-functor, we must prove we have strict preservation of
vertical and horizontal composition of 2-cells. Therefore, let
\omega\colon G\to H
and
\xi\colon H\to K
be 2-cells, with
H,\,K

composable with
F,\,G
respectively. We have
\mathfrak{F}(\omega\circ\theta)_{0}=\mathfrak{F}(\omega)_{0}\circ\mathfrak{F}(\theta)_{0}
and
\mathfrak{F}(\xi\cdot\theta)_{0}=\mathfrak{F}(\xi)_{0}\cdot\mathfrak{F}(\theta)_{0}
. Moreover, given a composable string of
horizontal arrows
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(\omega\circ\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}

\displaystyle=((\omega\circ\theta)_{r_{1}},\ldots,(\omega\circ\theta)_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle=(\omega_{r_{1}},\ldots,\omega_{r_{n}})\circ(\theta_{r_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{F}(\omega)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}\circ\mathfrak{F}(\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(\xi\cdot\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}

\displaystyle=((\xi\cdot\theta)_{r_{1}},\ldots,(\xi\cdot\theta)_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle=(\xi_{r_{1}},\ldots,\xi_{r_{n}})\cdot(\theta_{r_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{F}(\xi)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}\cdot\mathfrak{F}(\theta)_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}},

as desired. Nothing needs to be done to verify that
m,e
are 2-natural
transformations.

A pseudodouble category consists of a graph of categories
\mathbb{D}=(\mathbb{D}_{1}\rightrightarrows\mathbb{D}_{0})
, with vertical domain and
codomain functors. The algebra structure
a\colon\mathfrak{F}\mathbb{D}\to\mathbb{D}
is the identity on 0-cells and vertical 1-cells. We define
a()=1

(at 0-cells), and if
a
is defined for
\mathbb{D}^{(n)}
, we define

a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1})=r_{n+1}\cdot a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}).

We let
\eta\colon\mathsf{id}\to a\cdot e
be the identity on
\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{D}_{0}}
, and
\eta_{r}\colon r\to a(r)
is given by

\rho^{-1}\colon r\to r\cdot 1=r\cdot a()=a(r).

We define
\mu\colon a\cdot\mathfrak{F}a\to a\cdot m
to be the identity on

\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{D}_{0}}
, and on
\mathfrak{F}\mathfrak{F}\mathbb{D}_{1}\to\mathfrak{F}\mathbb{D}_{1}
by
double induction:

\displaystyle\mu_{()}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{id},

\displaystyle\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},0}

\displaystyle=\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\circ\lambda,

\displaystyle\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}+1}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ\alpha

where

\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\colon a(a(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{1,k_{1}}),\ldots,a(r_{n,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}))\to a(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}).

To prove that
(\mathbb{D},a,\eta,\mu)
is a pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra, we must
verify that

\displaystyle\mu_{m}\circ\eta_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{m})}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{id},

\displaystyle\mu_{1,\ldots,1}\circ a(\eta_{r_{1}},\ldots,\eta_{r_{m}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{id},

\displaystyle\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n,k_{n}}}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n})

where we use the following abbreviations:

\displaystyle\hat{j}_{i,k_{i}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{k_{i}}j_{i,p}

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}

\displaystyle=\mu_{j_{p,1},\ldots,j_{p,k_{p}}}

For the first and second, we argue by induction. When
m=0
, the first
becomes
\lambda_{1}\circ\rho^{-1}_{1}=\mathsf{id}
, and the second trivializes.
If we assume the equations hold for some
m
, then

\mu_{m+1}\circ\rho^{-1}=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{m})\circ\alpha\circ\rho^{-1}=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{m})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})=\mathsf{id},

and

\displaystyle\mu_{1,\ldots,1,1}\circ a(\rho^{-1},\ldots,\rho^{-1},\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{1,\ldots,1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\lambda)\circ\alpha\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot a(\rho^{-1},\ldots,\rho^{-1}))

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{1,\ldots,1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot a(\rho^{-1},\ldots,\rho^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{id}

For the third, we use triple induction. If
n=0
, it trivializes, so we
assume it holds for some
n
. If
k_{n+1}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},k_{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\circ\lambda

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n,k_{n}}}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n})\circ\lambda

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n,k_{n}},0}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n,k_{n}},\hat{j}_{n+1,k_{n+1}}}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\sigma_{n+1}).

Now, we assume it holds for some
k_{n+1}
. If
j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}=0
,
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}},0}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},k_{n+1}+1}

\displaystyle=\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}}}\circ\lambda\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}}\circ\lambda\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n+1,k_{n+1}}}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n+1})\circ(\lambda\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,(\sigma_{n+1}\circ\lambda)),

and finally, if we assume it holds for some
j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}
, then we
have

\displaystyle\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}+1}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}+1}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ\alpha\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}}))\circ\alpha\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)\circ\alpha\circ(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{j_{1,1},\ldots,j_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}+1})\circ\alpha\circ(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n+1,k_{n+1}}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n+1}))\circ\alpha\circ(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n+1,k_{n+1}}})\circ\alpha\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma_{n+1})\cdot a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))\circ(\alpha\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\hat{j}_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,\hat{j}_{n+1,k_{n+1}}+1}\circ a(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma_{n+1})\circ\alpha),

so the result holds by induction. It should be noted that the proof (so far)
remains unchanged if we consider left-biased double categories, in which case

(\mathbb{D},a,\eta,\mu)
is a lax
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra instead. Respectively,
right-biased double categories
\to
oplax
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras.

If we have a lax functor
F\colon\mathbb{D}\to\mathbb{E}
between ordinary
double categories, we define a pseudo
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra lax morphism
(F,\gamma^{F})\colon(\mathbb{D},a,\eta,\mu)\to(\mathbb{E},b,\eta,\mu)
, taking

F
to be the same underlying graph morphism, and we define
\gamma^{F}\colon b\circ\mathfrak{F}F\to F\circ a
inductively as follows:

\displaystyle\gamma^{F}_{x}

\displaystyle=\unit^{F}_{x},

\displaystyle\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\mult^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}).

to confirm
(F,\gamma^{F})
is indeed a lax morphism, we will prove that

F\eta=\gamma_{r}\circ\eta

and

\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}=F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\circ\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}}\circ b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}),

where

\displaystyle s_{i}

\displaystyle=a(r_{i,1},\ldots,r_{i,k_{i}}),

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{r_{i,1},\ldots,r_{i,k_{i}}}.

The first is just a restatement of the coherence diagram for the right unitor.
For the second, when
n=0
, the equation is trivial;
\unit^{F}=\unit^{F}
. Now,
we assume the equation holds for some
n
. If
k_{n+1}=0
, then

\displaystyle\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},0}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\circ\lambda

\displaystyle=F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\circ\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}}\circ b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n})\circ\lambda

\displaystyle=\lambda\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))

\displaystyle=F\lambda\circ\mult^{F}\circ(\unit^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))

\displaystyle=F\lambda\circ F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}})\circ\mult^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}})\circ(\unit^{F}\cdot b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))

\displaystyle=F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},0}\circ\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n},s_{n+1}}\circ b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\sigma_{n+1}),

so, we assume the identity holds for some
k_{n+1}
. We have

\displaystyle\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}\circ\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},k_{n+1}+1}

\displaystyle\quad=\mult^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n+1,k_{n+1}}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\quad=\mult^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n},s_{n+1}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\sigma_{n+1}))\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\quad=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ\mult^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{F})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}}))\circ\alpha\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma_{n+1})\cdot b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))

\displaystyle\quad=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}})\circ F\alpha\circ\mult^{F}\circ(\mult^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma_{n+1})\cdot b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}))

\displaystyle\quad=F\mu_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}+1}\circ\gamma_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n+1}}\circ b(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\mult^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma_{n+1})),

so, the result follows by induction.

This assignment preserves maps identities to identities (trivially), and
preserves composition; that is, this defines a functor
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}\to\PsfcAlg_{\mathsf{lax}}
. To see this, let
G\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{C}
be
another lax functor. We have

\displaystyle(G\gamma^{F}\circ\gamma^{G})_{()}

\displaystyle=G\unit^{F}\circ e^{G},

\displaystyle(G\gamma^{F}\circ\gamma^{G})_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\mult^{GF}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot G\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{G}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n}})

\displaystyle=G\mult^{F}\circ\mult^{G}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot G\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{G}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n}})

\displaystyle=G\mult^{F}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})\circ\mult^{G}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{G}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n}})

\displaystyle=G(\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1}})\circ\gamma^{G}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n+1}}.

We claim the functor
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}\to\PsfcAlg_{\mathsf{lax}}
is fully faithful;
if
(F,\gamma^{F})\colon(\mathbb{D},a,\eta,\mu)\to(\mathbb{E},b,\eta,\mu)

is a lax morphism between (the image of) double categories, we define

\displaystyle\unit^{F}_{x}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{F}_{x}

\displaystyle\mult^{F}_{r,s}

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{r,s}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

We must confirm these satisfy the coherence conditions. First, we observe that

\displaystyle\mult^{F}_{1,s}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{F})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{1,s}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{F})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\lambda)\circ F(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\gamma^{F}_{1,a(s)}\circ(F\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\unit^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\lambda^{-1})

\displaystyle=F\mu_{0,1}\circ\gamma^{F}_{1,a(s)}\circ b(\gamma^{F}_{()},\gamma^{F}_{s})\circ\mu^{-1}_{0,1}=\gamma_{s}^{F},

\displaystyle\mult^{F}_{r,1}\circ(\unit^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{r,1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\unit^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ F(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\gamma^{F}_{a(r),1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(F\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\unit^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{a(r),1}\circ b(\gamma^{F}_{r},\gamma^{F}_{()})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ F\mu_{1,0}^{-1}\circ\gamma^{F}_{r}\circ\mu_{1,0}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=F\lambda^{-1}\circ F\rho\circ\gamma^{F}_{r}\circ\rho^{-1}\circ\lambda

\displaystyle=F\lambda^{-1}\circ\lambda

which gives the unit comparsion coherences for
F
, and after calculating

\displaystyle\mu_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\alpha\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\cdot(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id}))

\displaystyle\mu_{2,1}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id})

we verify that

\displaystyle F\alpha

\displaystyle\circ\mult^{F}_{r,t\cdot s}\circ(\mult^{F}_{s,t}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=F\alpha\circ F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{r,t\cdot s}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\gamma^{F}_{s,t}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=F\alpha\circ F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ F((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\cdot(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\gamma_{a(r),a(s,t)}^{F}\circ(\gamma^{F}_{s,t}\cdot(F\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho))\circ F\alpha\circ F((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\cdot(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\gamma^{F}_{a(r),a(s,t)}\circ b(\gamma^{F}_{r},\gamma^{F}_{s,t})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\cdot(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho))\circ\gamma^{F}_{r,s,t}\circ\alpha\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle\mult^{F}_{s\cdot r,t}

\displaystyle\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{F}_{r,s})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{s\cdot r,t}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{F}_{r,s})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1}))\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ F(\rho\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\gamma^{F}_{a(r,s),a(t)}\circ(F\rho^{-1}\cdot(F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{F}_{r,s})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1}))\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho))\circ F(\rho\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma_{a(r,s),a(t)}^{F}\circ b(\gamma^{F}_{r,s},\gamma^{F}_{t})\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1}))\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho))\circ\gamma^{F}_{r,s,t}\circ\alpha\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

which confirms coherence for the associator comparison. We further verify
that, by induction,
\mu_{n,1}\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\rho^{-1})=\mathsf{id}

(pattern matching), so that

\displaystyle\mult^{F}_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}),r_{n+1}}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})

\displaystyle=F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}),r_{n+1}}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})

\displaystyle=F(\rho\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{F}_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}),a(r_{n+1})}\circ b(\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}},\gamma^{F}_{r_{n+1}})\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=\gamma^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1}},

confirming that the functor
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}\to\PsfcAlg_{\mathsf{lax}}
is fully
faithful.

We claim the functor
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}\to\PsfcAlg_{\mathsf{lax}}
is essentially
surjective; let
(\mathbb{D},a,\eta,\mu)
be a pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra. We
define

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=a(),

\displaystyle s\cdot r

\displaystyle=a(r,s),

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}

\displaystyle=\eta^{-1}_{r}\circ\mu_{r,-}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}),

\displaystyle\rho_{r}

\displaystyle=\eta^{-1}_{r}\circ\mu_{-,r}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{r}),

\displaystyle\alpha_{r,s,t}

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu_{rs,t}^{-1}\circ\mu_{r,st}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}).

These endow
\mathbb{D}
with the structure of a double category; to see
this, we must verify the coherence conditions hold. First, we have

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}\cdot\lambda_{r})\circ\alpha_{r,1,s}

\displaystyle=a(\eta^{-1}_{r},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\mu_{r,-},\mathsf{id})\circ a(a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}),\mathsf{id})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s})\circ\mu_{r1,s}^{-1}\circ\mu_{r,1s}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\eta^{-1}_{r},\eta^{-1}_{s})\circ a(\mu_{r,-},\mathsf{id})\circ a(a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}),\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{r1,s}^{-1}\circ\mu_{r,1s}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{r,s}\circ a(\mu_{r,-},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{a(r)1,s}^{-1}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{r,1s}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{r,s}\circ a(\mu_{r,-},\mu_{s})\circ\mu_{a(r)1,a(s)}^{-1}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id},\eta_{s})\circ\mu_{r,1s}\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{r,-,s}\circ\mu_{a(r),1a(s)}\circ a(a(\eta_{r}),a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}))\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{r,s}\circ a(\mu_{r},\mu_{-,s})\circ a(a(\eta_{r}),a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}))\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\eta_{r}^{-1},\eta_{s}^{-1})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{-,s})\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}))\circ a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}^{-1})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{-,s})\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}))=\rho_{s}\cdot\mathsf{id}.

and for the associator pentagon, we have, on one hand

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha_{r,s,t})\circ\alpha_{q,a(r,s),t}\circ(\alpha_{q,r,s}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\quad=a(a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu^{-1}_{qr,s},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\mu_{q,rs},\mathsf{id})\circ a(a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu_{qa(r,s),t}^{-1}\circ\mu_{q,a(r,s)t}\circ a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t}))\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{rs,t}^{-1})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{r,st})\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}))

\displaystyle\quad=a(a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s}),\eta^{-1}_{t})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu^{-1}_{qr,s},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\mu_{q,rs},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id}),\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{qa(r,s),t}^{-1}\circ\mu_{q,a(r,s)t}\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t}))

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{rs,t}^{-1})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{r,st})\circ a(\eta_{q},a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}))

\displaystyle\quad=a(a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s}),\eta^{-1}_{t})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu^{-1}_{qr,s},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\mu_{q,rs},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\eta^{-1}_{t}))\circ\mu_{a(q)a(r,s),a(t)}^{-1}\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r,s)a(t)}\circ a(a(\eta_{q}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{rs,t}^{-1})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{r,st})\circ a(\eta_{q},a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}))

\displaystyle\quad=a(a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s}),\eta^{-1}_{t})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu^{-1}_{qr,s},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\mu_{q,rs},\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\circ\mu_{a(q)a(r,s),a(t)}^{-1}\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r,s)a(t)}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{q}^{-1},\mu_{rs,t}^{-1})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{r,st})\circ a(\eta_{q},a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id}))

\displaystyle\quad=a(a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s}),\eta^{-1}_{t})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu^{-1}_{qr,s},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{qrs,t}^{-1}

\displaystyle\circ\mu_{q,rst}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{r,st})\circ a(\eta_{q},a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})),

while on the other, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{a(q,r),s,t}\circ\alpha_{q,r,a(s,t)}

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)s,t}\circ\mu_{a(q,r),st}\circ a(\eta_{a(q,r)},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\eta_{s},\eta_{t}))\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{a(s,t)})\circ a(\eta_{a(q,r)},\mathsf{id})\circ a(a(\eta_{q},\eta_{r}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q)a(r),a(s,t)}\circ a(\eta_{q}^{-1},\eta_{r}^{-1},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{q,ra(s,t)}\circ a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)s,t}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}^{-1},\eta_{r}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{a(q,r),a(s)a(t)}\circ a(\eta_{a(q,r)},a(\eta_{s},\eta_{t}))

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(a(\eta_{q}^{-1},\eta_{r}^{-1}),\eta^{-1}_{a(s,t)})\circ\mu_{a(q)a(r),a(s,t)}

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(\eta_{q},\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{q,ra(s,t)}\circ a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)s,t}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}^{-1},\eta_{r}^{-1})\circ\mu_{qr,s,t}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{q,r,st}\circ a(\eta_{q},\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{q,ra(s,t)}\circ a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)s,t}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}^{-1},\eta_{r}^{-1})\circ\mu_{qr,s,t}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{q,r,st}\circ a(\eta_{q},\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{q,ra(s,t)}\circ a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)s,t}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{s}^{-1},\eta_{r}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(\mu_{q,r},\mu_{s},\mu_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q)a(r),a(s),a(t)}

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r),a(s)a(t)}\circ a(\mu_{q}^{-1},\mu_{r}^{-1},\mu_{s,t}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(\eta_{q},\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})\circ\mu_{q,ra(s,t)}\circ a(\eta_{q},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=a(a(\mathsf{id},\eta^{-1}_{s}),\eta^{-1}_{t})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)a(s),a(t)}

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(\mu_{q,r},\mu_{s},\mu_{t})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q)a(r),a(s),a(t)}

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r),a(s)a(t)}\circ a(\mu_{q}^{-1},\mu_{r}^{-1},\mu_{s,t}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r)a(s,t)}\circ a(\mu_{q},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\eta_{q},a(\eta_{r},\mathsf{id})),

so, our goal is to prove that

(4.1)

\displaystyle a(\mu^{-1}_{qr,s},\mu_{t}^{-1})\circ\mu_{qrs,t}^{-1}\circ\mu_{q,rst}\circ a(\mu_{q},\mu_{r,st})

\displaystyle=\mu^{-1}_{a(q,r)a(s),a(t)}\circ a(\mu_{q,r},\mu_{s},\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(q)a(r),a(s),a(t)}\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r),a(s)a(t)}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{q}^{-1},\mu_{r}^{-1},\mu_{s,t}^{-1})\circ\mu_{a(q),a(r)a(s,t)}.

And to do so, we observe that the following diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(a(q)),a(a(r),a(s,t)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(q),a(r,s,t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(q),a(r),a(s,t))}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(q),a(r),a(s),a(t))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{a(q),a(r)a(s,t)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(\mu_{q},\mu_{r,st})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{q,rst}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(\mu_{q}^{-1},\mu_{r}^{-1},\mu_{s,t}^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{q,r,st}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{q,r,s,t}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{a(q),a(r),a(s)a(t)}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(a(q,r),a(s)),a(a(t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(q,r,s,t)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(q,r),a(s),a(t))}
