{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(a(q),a(r)),a(a(s)),a(a(t)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(\mu_{qr,s}^{-1},\mu_{t}^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{qr,s,t}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{qrs,t}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{a(q,r)a(s),a(t)}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{q,r,s,t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{a(q)a(r),a(s),a(t)}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(\mu_{q,r},\mu_{s},\mu_{t})}

are pastings of associativity squares for
\mu
, and are therefore
commutative. Pasting these diagrams along
\mu_{q,r,s,t}
will confirm
(
4.1
), and we conclude that
\mathbb{D}
has the structure
of a pseudodouble category.

Now, write
(\mathbb{D},\overline{a},\overline{\eta},\overline{\mu})
for the
pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra induced by the above double category. We define
\gamma\colon\overline{a}\to a
to be the natural transformation inductively
given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{()}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{id},

\displaystyle\gamma_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n},r_{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n},r_{n+1}}\circ a(\gamma_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}},\eta_{r_{n+1}})

We claim that
(\mathsf{id},\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{D},a,\eta,\mu)\to(\mathbb{D},\overline{a},\overline{\eta},\overline{\mu})
is an invertible lax morphism of
pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras. First, note that

\gamma_{r}\circ\overline{\eta}_{r}=\mu_{-,r}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{r})\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{r}^{-1})\circ\mu_{-,r}^{-1}\circ\eta_{r}=\eta_{r},

and we shall prove that

(4.2)

\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}=\mu_{(r_{1,i})_{i=1}^{k_{1}},\ldots,(r_{n,i})_{i=1}^{k_{n}}}\circ\gamma_{a(r_{1,i})_{i=1}^{k_{1}},\ldots,(r_{n,i})_{i=1}^{k_{n}}}\circ\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{1,k_{1}}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}})

by double induction. When
n=0
, the above reduces to

\mathsf{id}=\mu_{()}\circ a(\mathsf{id}),

which holds, so assume the above is true for some
n
. If
k_{n+1}=0
,
the left-hand side of (
4.2
) becomes

\displaystyle\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n},0}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}}\circ\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}}\circ\lambda

\displaystyle=\lambda\circ a(\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}},\mathsf{id})

while the right-hand side of (
4.2
) becomes

\displaystyle\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n,i}),()}

\displaystyle\circ\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})}\circ\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n,i}),()}\circ\mu_{a(r_{1,i})\cdots a(r_{n,i}),a()}\circ a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})},\eta_{a()})\circ a(\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n,i},()}\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n,i})},\mu_{(())})\circ a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})},\eta_{a()})\circ a(\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n,i},()}\circ a(\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}},\mathsf{id})\circ a(\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}},\mathsf{id})

so the equality holds by verifying that

\mu_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n},()}=\lambda,

which is equivalent to proving that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})),a())}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}),a())}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(\mu_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n}}{,}\mathsf{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}),()}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{r_{1}\cdots r_{n},()}}

which is given by the coherence condition
\mu\circ\mathfrak{F}\mu=\mu\circ\mu_{\mathfrak{F}}
; recall that
\mu_{()}=\mathsf{id}
.

Hence, for the final induction step, we suppose (
4.2
) holds
for some
k_{n+1}
. For
k_{n+1}+1
, the left-hand side of
(
4.2
) is given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}\circ\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}+1}

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n+1,k_{n+1}}},\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})

\displaystyle\circ a(\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n+1}},\mathsf{id})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n+1,i})}\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{a(r_{1,i}\cdots r_{n,i}),a(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})},\eta_{a(r_{n+1,i})}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(a(\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}}),\gamma_{r_{n+1},i}),id)

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots(r_{n,i})},\mu_{(r_{n+1,i})}),\mathsf{id})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})},a(\eta_{a(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id}))

\displaystyle\circ a(\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}}),a(\gamma_{r_{n+1},i},\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}))

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\gamma_{r_{n+1},i},\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})),

while the right-hand side is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n,i}),(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\circ\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i}),a(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}\circ\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}},\gamma_{r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n,i}),(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\circ\mu_{a(r_{1,i})\cdots a(r_{n,i}),a(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})},\eta_{a(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})})

\displaystyle\circ a(\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}}),\gamma_{r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}),\ldots,(r_{n,i})},\mu_{(r_{n+1,i}r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})})

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{a(r_{1,i}),\ldots,a(r_{n,i})},\eta_{a(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})})

\displaystyle\circ a(\overline{a}(\gamma_{r_{1,i}},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{n,i}}),\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{(r_{n+1,i}),r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\gamma_{r_{n+1,i}},\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}))

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\circ a(\gamma_{r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\overline{\mu}_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}},\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{(r_{n+1,i}),r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})

\displaystyle\circ a(\mathsf{id},a(\gamma_{r_{n+1,i}},\eta_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})),

and therefore, proving (
4.2
) reduces to verifying that

\displaystyle\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i})},\mathsf{id})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}\circ a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{(r_{n+1,i}),r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}).

Here, we have

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=a(\mathsf{id},\mu_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}})a(r_{n+1,i}),a(r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\quad\circ\mu_{a(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}),a(r_{n+1,i})a(r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\quad\circ a(\mu^{-1}_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}}},\mathsf{id}),

so we just need to verify that

\displaystyle\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n+1,k_{n+1}},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}}\circ a(\mu_{(r_{1,i}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i})},\mu_{r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}})a(r_{n+1,i}),a(r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}),(r_{n+1,i},r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})}\circ a(\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}}}\mu_{(r_{n+1,i}),r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}})\circ\mu^{-1}_{a(r_{1,1},\ldots,r_{n,k_{n}}),a(r_{n+1,i})a(r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1})},

which holds, since both sides of the above expression equal

\mu_{r_{1,1}\cdots r_{n,k_{n}},(r_{n+1,i}),r_{n+1,k_{n+1}+1}},

confirming that
(\mathsf{id},\gamma)
is an invertible pseudo-morphism of
pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras.

We consider double categories, lax (horizontal) and oplax (vertical) functors
as in the following diagram, and the respective diagram in the double category

\PsfcAlg
.

(4.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A},a,\eta,\mu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{B},b,\eta,\mu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{C},c,\eta,\mu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{D},d,\eta,\mu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F,\delta^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma^{H})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\delta^{G})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\gamma^{K})}

Let
\omega
be a 2-cell
GH\to KF
of internal
\mathsf{Cat}
-graphs.
The claim is that
\omega
is a generalized vertical transformation if and
only if
\omega
is a generalized 2-cell of pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebras.

If
\omega
is a generalized vertical transformation, we wish to prove
that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gb(Hr_{1},\ldots,Hr_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d(GHr_{1},\ldots,GHr_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GHa(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d(KFr_{1},\ldots,KFr_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KFa(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kc(Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{G}_{Hr_{1},\ldots,Hr_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\gamma^{H}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d(\omega_{r_{1}},\ldots,\omega_{r_{n}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{K}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\delta^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}}

For all
n
and all horizontal 1-cells
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}
. We proceed by
induction: when
n=0
, the above is just coherence of
\omega
for the
unit comparsion. If true for some
n
, then

\displaystyle K\delta^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1}}

\displaystyle\circ\omega_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1})}\circ G\gamma^{H}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n+1}}

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})\circ K\mult^{F}\circ\omega_{r_{n+1}\cdot a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})}\circ G\mult^{H}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{H}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots r_{n}})\circ\mult^{K}\circ(\omega_{r_{n+1}}\cdot\omega_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})})\circ\mult^{G}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{H}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})

\displaystyle=\mult^{K}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot K\delta^{F}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})\circ(\omega_{r_{n+1}}\cdot\omega_{a(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot G\gamma^{H}_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}})\circ\mult^{G}

\displaystyle=\mult^{K}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\gamma^{K}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n}})\circ(\omega_{r_{n+1}}\cdot d(\omega_{r_{1}},\ldots,\omega_{r_{n}}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta^{G}_{Hr_{1},\ldots,Hr_{n}})\circ\mult^{G}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{K}_{Fr_{1},\ldots,Fr_{n+1}}\circ d(\omega_{r_{1}},\ldots,\omega_{r_{n+1}})\circ\delta^{G}_{Hr_{1},\ldots,Hr_{n+1}},

so
\omega
is a pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra 2-cell as well.

Now, if
\omega
is a pseudo-
\mathfrak{F}
-algebra 2-cell, the coherence for the
unit comparison holds by definition, and

\displaystyle\mult^{K}_{Fr,Fs}

\displaystyle\circ(\omega_{s}\cdot\omega_{r})\circ\mult^{G}_{Hr,Hs}

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{K}_{Fr,Fs}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ(\omega_{s}\cdot\omega_{r})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\delta^{G}_{Hr,Hs}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{K}_{Fr,Fs}\circ(\omega_{s}\cdot(\omega_{r}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\delta^{G}_{Hr,Hs}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\gamma^{K}_{Fr,Fs}\circ d(\omega_{r},\omega_{s})\circ\delta^{G}_{Hr,Hs}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ K\delta^{F}_{r,s}\circ\omega_{a(r,s)}\circ G\gamma^{H}_{r,s}\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=K(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ K\delta^{F}_{r,s}\circ KF(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ\omega_{s\cdot r}\circ GH(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ G(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=K(\mult^{F})\circ\omega_{s\cdot r}\circ G(\mult^{H}),

verifies coherence for composition comparsion, completing our proof.

Now, as promised at the start of Section
2
, we obtain:

Proposition 4.2
.

We have a conjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Span(\mathcal{V})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\boldsymbol{\cdot}1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}(1{,}-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

in the double category
\mathsf{PsDbCat}
.

Proof.

Via the equivalence
\mathsf{PsDbCat}\simeq\PsfcAlg
, we simply apply
Proposition
3.1
to the adjunction
-\boldsymbol{\cdot}1\dashv\mathcal{V}(1,-)
in
\mathsf{Grph}(\mathsf{Cat})
, with the oplax functor
structure of
-\boldsymbol{\cdot}1\colon\Span(\mathcal{V})\to\mathcal{V}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}
, all of which were
described in Section
2
.
∎

5.
Horizontal lax algebras and change of base

We will review the notion of categories of
horizontal lax algebras

introduced in
[
15
]
, and we define the
change-of-base
functors
between such categories, induced by an appropriate notion of monad morphism.
We begin by fixing monads
S=(\mathbb{D},S,m,e)
and
T=(\mathbb{E},T,m,e)
in the 2-category
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}
.

We define the category
\LaxTHAlg
of
horizontal lax

T
-algebras
, as follows:

–

Objects are given by 4-tuples
(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)
where
x
is a
0-cell,
a\colon Tx\nrightarrow x
is a horizontal 1-cell, and
\upsilon,\mu
are 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

satisfying

\displaystyle\mu\circ(\upsilon\cdot e_{a})

\displaystyle=\lambda

\displaystyle\mu\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(T\upsilon\circ\unit^{T}))

\displaystyle=\rho

\displaystyle\mu\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(T\mu\circ\mult^{T}))

\displaystyle=\mu\circ(\mu\cdot m_{a})\circ\alpha^{-1}

–

A morphism
(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)\to(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)
is a pair
(f,\zeta)
where
f\colon x\to y
is a vertical 1-cell and
\zeta
is a 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ty}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta}

satisfying
\zeta\circ\upsilon=\upsilon\circ 1_{f}
and
\zeta\circ\mu=\mu\circ(\zeta\cdot T\zeta)
.

It should be noted that
\mathsf{id}=(\mathsf{id},\mathsf{id})\colon(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)\to(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)
is a horizontal lax
T
-algebra morphism, and if
(f,\zeta)
,
(g,\xi)
are composable horizontal lax
T
-algebra
morphisms, then so is
(g,\xi)\circ(f,\zeta)=(g\circ f,\xi\circ\zeta)
. Associativity and identity properties are inherited from
\mathbb{E}_{0}
and
\mathbb{E}_{1}
, making
\LaxTHAlg
into a category.

Our work focuses on the cases
\mathbb{E}=\Span(\mathcal{V})
, with
T
induced by a
cartesian monad (also denoted by
T
) on
\mathcal{V}
, and
\mathbb{D}=\mathcal{V}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}
with
S
a lax monad. Then,
\LaxTHAlg=\mathsf{Cat}(T,\,\mathcal{V})
is the category
of
internal
T
-categories
of
[
22
]
, while
\LaxSHAlg=(S,\,\mathcal{V})\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}
is a generalization of the category of
enriched

S
-categories
introduced by
[
12
]
, by not requiring
S
to be
normal.
3
3

3

When
\mathcal{V}
is a quantale, this generalization is already
present in
[
40
]
.

Let
(F,\phi)\colon S\to T
be a monad oplax morphism, and we assume
\mathbb{E}
is conjoint closed. By Theorem
3.6
,
\phi
has a conjoint, given by a lax horizontal transformation
\phi^{*}\colon TF\to FS
. We define a 2-cell
\unit^{\phi^{*}_{x}}
for each 0-cell
x

given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{Fx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fe_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\unit^{\phi^{*}_{x}}}

as the mate of the commutative square
\phi_{x}\circ Fe_{x}=e_{Fx}\circ\mathsf{id}
, and a 2-cell
\mult^{\phi^{*}_{x}}
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\phi_{x})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{Sx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\phi_{x}\circ\phi_{Sx})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{Fx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fm_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1^{\lor}}

where
\pi
is given as in (
3.2
), and
1^{\lor}
is the
mate of the commutative square
\phi_{x}\circ Fm_{x}=m_{Fx}\circ(T\phi_{x}\circ\phi_{Sx})
. To be explicit, via mate correspondence we have

(5.1)

\varepsilon\circ\unit^{\phi^{*}_{x}}=1_{e_{Fx}},\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}_{x}}=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\rho\circ((1_{T\phi_{x}}\circ\varepsilon)\cdot\varepsilon).

Analogously, when
\mathbb{D}
is companion closed, we define 2-cells

\unit^{\psi_{!}}_{y}
and
\mult^{\psi_{!}}_{y}
for a monad lax morphism
(G,\psi)\colon T\to S
.

Lemma 5.1
.

If
(F,\phi)\colon S\to T
is a monad oplax morphism and
\mathbb{E}
is conjoint closed, then
\unit^{\phi^{*}}
and
\mult^{\phi^{*}}
are
modifications, and the following relations hold:

(a)

\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot e_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})=\lambda
,

(b)

\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\phi^{*}_{e}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})=\rho
,

(c)

\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot m_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})=\mult^{\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ(\phi^{*}_{m}\cdot\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\circ\alpha
,

where
e^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}}
and
m^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}}
are the mates of the
naturality squares of
e
and
m
at
\phi_{x}
, and
\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x}
,
\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}_{x}}
are respectively given by the mate
of the commutative square
T\phi_{x}\circ TFe_{x}=Te_{Fx}\circ\mathsf{id}
, and
the mate of the commutative square
T\phi_{x}\circ TFm_{x}=Tm_{Fx}\circ(TT\phi_{x}\circ T\phi_{Sx})
composed with
\pi
, satisfying
properties similar to (
5.1
).

Proof.

Note that
\pi
is given as a 2-cell (modification) in
\Lax(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{E})_{\mathsf{lax}}
, and
\unit^{\phi^{*}}
and
1^{\lor}
are mates of
equations of vertical 1-cells. It follows that
\unit^{\phi^{*}}
and
\mult^{\phi^{*}}
are modifications.

We have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot e_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})

\displaystyle=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\lambda\circ((1_{T\phi_{x}}\circ\varepsilon)\cdot\varepsilon)\circ(\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot e_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})

\displaystyle=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\lambda\circ(1_{T\phi_{x}\circ e_{FSx}}\cdot(1_{e_{TFx}}\circ_{\varepsilon}))

\displaystyle=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ 1_{e_{TFx}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\lambda=\varepsilon\circ\lambda,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\phi^{*}_{e}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})

\displaystyle=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\rho\circ((1_{T\phi_{x}}\circ\varepsilon)\cdot\varepsilon)\circ(\phi^{*}_{e}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})

\displaystyle=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\rho\circ((1_{T\phi_{x}\circ TFe_{x}}\circ\varepsilon)\cdot 1_{Te_{Fx}})

\displaystyle=1_{m_{Fx}}\circ 1_{Te_{Fx}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\rho=\varepsilon\circ\rho,

Now, we note that

(5.2)

\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot m_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})=\rho\circ\big((1_{m_{Fx}\circ T\phi_{x}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx})\cdot(1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\varepsilon\circ m_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})\big),

and we note that

\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}=\rho\circ\big((1_{m_{FSx}\circ T\phi_{Sx}}\circ\varepsilon)\cdot(1_{m_{FSx}}\circ\varepsilon)\big),

and

\varepsilon\circ m_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor}=1_{m_{FSx}}\circ\varepsilon,

so that (
5.2
) becomes

\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ(\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot m_{\phi_{x}}^{\lor})=\rho\circ(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((A\cdot B)\cdot C)

where
A=1_{\hat{A}}\circ\varepsilon
,
B=1_{\hat{B}}\circ\varepsilon
,
C=1_{\hat{C}}\circ\varepsilon
, and

–

\hat{A}=m_{Fx}\circ T\phi_{x}\circ m_{FSx}\circ T\phi_{Sx}
,

–

\hat{B}=m_{Fx}\circ T\phi_{x}\circ m_{FSx}
,

–

\hat{C}=m_{Fx}\circ m_{TFx}
.

On the other hand, we have

(5.3)

\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ(\phi^{*}_{m}\cdot\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\circ\alpha=\rho\circ\big((1_{m_{Fx}\circ T\phi_{x}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\phi^{*}_{m})\cdot(1_{m_{Fx}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\big),

and we note that

\varepsilon\circ\phi^{*}_{m}=1_{TFm_{x}}\circ\varepsilon,

and

\varepsilon\circ\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x}=\lambda\circ\big((1_{Tm_{Fx}\circ TT\phi_{x}}\circ\varepsilon)\cdot(1_{Tm_{Fx}}\circ\varepsilon)\big),

so that (
5.3
) becomes

\varepsilon\circ\mult^{\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ(\phi^{*}_{m}\cdot\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\circ\alpha=\rho\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\lambda)\circ\alpha\circ((X\cdot Y)\cdot Z),

where
X=1_{\hat{X}}\circ\varepsilon
,
Y=1_{\hat{Y}}\circ\varepsilon
,
Z=1_{\hat{Z}}\circ\varepsilon
, and

–

\hat{X}=m_{Fx}\circ T\phi_{x}\circ TFm_{x}
,

–

\hat{Y}=m_{Fx}\circ Tm_{Fx}\circ TT\phi_{x}
,

–

\hat{Z}=m_{Fx}\circ Tm_{Fx}
.

We conclude the proof by observing that
A=X
,
B=Y
and
C=Z
.
∎

Theorem 5.2
.

We suppose that
(F,\phi)\colon S\to T
is an monad oplax morphism and
that
\mathbb{E}
is conjoint closed. If
\phi
and
T\phi
have
strong conjoints (see Lemma
3.7
), then
(F,\phi)
induces a functor
F_{!}\colon\LaxSHAlg\to\LaxTHAlg
.

Analogously, if we suppose that
(G,\psi)\colon T\to S
is a monad
lax morphism and that
\mathbb{D}
is companion closed, then
(G,\psi)
induces a functor
G_{!}\colon\LaxTHAlg\to\LaxSHAlg
.

Proof.

The functors
F_{!}
and
G_{!}
are given on objects by

F_{!}(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)=(Fx,Fa\cdot\phi_{x}^{*},F_{!}\upsilon,F_{!}\mu)\quad\text{and}\quad G_{!}(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)=(Gy,Gb\cdot\psi_{y!},G_{!}\upsilon,G_{!}\mu),

where
F_{!}\upsilon
,
F_{!}\mu
are respectively given by the
following 2-cells:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{Fx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fe_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{x}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\unit^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\upsilon}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSx}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\phi_{x})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TFa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\phi_{x})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{Fx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{Sx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FSa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot Sa)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fm_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\phi}^{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a})^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mu}

where
\theta_{\phi}^{T}
is the inverse of
\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})
, and
G_{!}\upsilon
,
G_{!}\mu
are respectively given by
the following 2-cells:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{Gy}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ge_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{y!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\unit^{\psi_{!}}_{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\unit^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\upsilon}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SSGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SSGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SSGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gy}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(Gb\cdot\psi_{y!})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{y!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gb}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S\psi_{y})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SGb}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{y!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S\psi_{t})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{Gx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{Tx!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GTb}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gb}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(b\cdot Tb)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gm_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{y!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\psi}^{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nat^{\psi_{!}}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{\psi_{!}}_{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mu}

where
\theta^{S}_{\psi}
is the inverse of
\mult^{S}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\tau^{S})
, given by Lemma
3.5
.

If
(f,\zeta)\colon(w,a,\upsilon,\mu)\to(x,b,\upsilon,\mu)
is a
horizontal lax
S
-algebra morphism, and if
(g,\xi)\colon(y,c,\upsilon,\mu)\to(z,d,\upsilon,\mu)
is a horizontal lax

T
-algebra morphism, then we have

F_{!}(f,\zeta)=(Ff,F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f})\quad\text{and}\quad G_{!}(g,\xi)=(Gg,G\xi\cdot\psi_{f!}).

We observe that
\phi
and
T\phi
are required to have strong
conjoints only to guarantee the existence of
F_{!}\mu
. All other things
being equal, up to a letter substitution, we conclude it is enough to verify
that one of
F_{!}(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)
,
G_{!}(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)
is a
horizontal lax algebra, and likewise for the morphisms.

Throughout the calculations, we will use the following abbreviations:

–

\upsilon^{F}=F\upsilon\circ\unit^{F}
,

–

\mu^{F}=F\mu\circ\mult^{F}
,

–

\hat{\alpha}=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha^{-1})\circ\alpha
,

–

\tilde{\theta}=\mathsf{id}\cdot(\theta\cdot\mathsf{id})
for a 2-cell
\theta
.

–

N^{\omega}_{p}=\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ({\tilde{\nat}}^{\omega}_{p})^{-1}\circ\hat{\alpha}
for a strong (lax) horizontal transformation
\omega
.

We begin by verifying that the following equalities hold:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\theta^{T}\circ e_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}

\displaystyle=e_{Fa}\cdot e^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}},

(5.5)

\displaystyle\theta^{T}\circ T(\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ\unit^{T}

\displaystyle=(\upsilon^{TF}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\circ\rho^{-1},

We obtain (
5.4
), via mate correspondence, by noting that

\displaystyle\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(e_{Fa}\cdot e^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ(e_{Fa}\cdot 1_{e_{FSx}})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\eta)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}\circ e_{Fa}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}\circ e_{Fa}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ e_{Fa}

\displaystyle=e_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\rho^{-1},

and (
5.5
), directly, since

\displaystyle\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(\upsilon^{TF}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\eta)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ(\upsilon^{TF}\cdot 1_{TFe_{x}})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}\circ\upsilon^{TF}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ\upsilon^{TF}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ T(\upsilon^{F}\cdot 1_{Fe_{x}})\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ\unit^{T}

\displaystyle=T(\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ\unit^{T}.

Furthermore, since
\unit^{\phi^{*}}
is a modification and
\nat^{\phi^{*}}

is natural, we respectively obtain

(5.6)

\displaystyle(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a})^{-1}\circ(\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ e_{Fa})

\displaystyle=(Fe_{a}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx})\circ\gamma,

(5.7)

\displaystyle(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a})^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\upsilon^{TF})

\displaystyle=(\upsilon^{FS}\cdot\phi^{*}_{e})\circ\gamma.

And lastly, we note that the following diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\cdot Fa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F1\cdot Fa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(1\cdot a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot FSa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot Sa)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\unit^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon^{F}\cdot Fe_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\upsilon\cdot Fe_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\upsilon\cdot e_{a})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mu}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot F1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot 1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot FSa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot Sa)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\upsilon^{FS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot F\upsilon^{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\upsilon^{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mu}

which respectively confirm that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mu^{F}\circ(\upsilon^{F}\cdot Fe_{a})

\displaystyle=\lambda,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mu^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\upsilon^{FS})

\displaystyle=\rho.

By applying (
5.4
), (
5.6
), (
5.8
),
and
(a)
from Lemma
5.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})

\displaystyle\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ\big((\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\cdot e_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}\big)\circ(\lambda^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\qquad=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\big((\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\cdot(e_{Fa}\cdot e^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}})\big)\circ(\lambda^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\qquad=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ\big((\upsilon^{F}\cdot Fe_{a})\cdot(\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot e^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}})\big)\circ N^{1}_{a}\circ(\lambda^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle\qquad=(\lambda\cdot\lambda)\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\gamma}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\lambda^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})=\lambda,

verifying the left identity law, and by applying (
5.5
),
(
5.7
), (
5.9
) and
(b)
from Lemma

5.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})

\displaystyle\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ\big(\mathsf{id}\cdot(T(\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}})\circ T\lambda^{-1}\circ\unit^{T})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\big(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\upsilon^{TF}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\big)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle\qquad=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ\big((\mathsf{id}\cdot\upsilon^{FS})\cdot(\phi^{*}_{e}\cdot\unit^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x})\big)\circ N^{1}_{a}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle\qquad=(\rho\cdot\rho)\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\gamma}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})=\rho,

verifying the right identity law.

Now, note that

(5.10)

\theta^{T}\circ m_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}=(m_{Fa}\cdot m^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}})\circ\theta^{TT}

holds via mate correspondence, since we have

\displaystyle m_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ\mult^{TT}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{TT})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=m_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ\mult^{TT}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot TT\eta)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{TT})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=m_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}\circ TT(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\mult^{TT}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{TT})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ m_{Fa\cdot 1}\circ TT\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ m_{Fa},

\displaystyle\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(m_{Fa}\cdot m^{\lor}_{\phi_{x}})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ(m_{Fa}\cdot 1)\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\eta)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}\circ m_{Fa}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}\circ m_{Fa}

\displaystyle=T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ T\rho^{-1}\circ m_{Fa},

and since
\mult^{\phi^{*}}
is a modification, we get

(5.11)

(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a})^{-1}\circ(\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\cdot m_{Fa})=(Fm_{a}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx})\circ(\nat^{\phi^{*}_{S}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})^{-1}.

Now, note that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fa\cdot FSa)\cdot FSSa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot(FSa\cdot FSSa)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot F(Sa\cdot SSa)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot FSa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot Sa)\cdot FSSa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((a\cdot Sa)\cdot SSa)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot(Sa\cdot SSa))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot Sa)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot FSa}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(a\cdot Sa)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}\cdot\mathsf{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot F\mu^{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mu\cdot Fm_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mu\cdot m_{a})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu^{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mu}

which confirms

(5.12)

\mu^{F}\circ(\mu^{F}\cdot Fm_{a})=\mu^{F}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu^{FS})\circ\alpha.

Our next step is to confirm that

(5.13)

((\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{FS})\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\circ N^{\phi^{*}_{S}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\circ(N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})=N^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mult^{TF}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ\alpha.

First, we recall that

\nat^{\phi^{*}_{S}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}=\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\nat^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ\alpha\circ(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{Sa}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\alpha^{-1},

and

\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\circ(\mult^{FS}\cdot\mathsf{id})=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{TF})\circ\alpha\circ(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{Sa})\circ\alpha,

and note that by coherence, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ\alpha,

\displaystyle(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha),

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)\circ\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha}^{-1})\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)

\displaystyle\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ\alpha\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ\alpha,

so that

\displaystyle((\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{FS})\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\circ N^{\phi^{*}_{S}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\circ(N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=((\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{FS})\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{Sa}\cdot\mathsf{id})\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\nat^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\cdot\mathsf{id})^{-1}\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mult^{FS}\cdot\mathsf{id})\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{Sa})\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)\circ\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\nat^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})))^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ\alpha\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mult^{FS}\cdot\mathsf{id})\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{Sa})\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\nat^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})))^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ\alpha\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\cdot\mathsf{id}))^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{TF})\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\tilde{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ\alpha\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=N^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mult^{TF}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ\alpha\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=N^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mult^{TF}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha}^{-1})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot{\tilde{\nat}}^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})^{-1}\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\hat{\alpha})\circ\alpha\circ(\hat{\alpha}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=N^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mult^{TF}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ\alpha.

Next, we observe that

(5.14)

(FS\mu\cdot\phi^{*}_{m})\circ(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa})^{-1}=(\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a})^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot TF\mu)

holds by naturality of
\nat^{\phi^{*}}
, and lastly we must confirm that

(5.15)

(\mu^{TF}\cdot\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\circ(\theta^{T}\cdot\theta^{TT})=\theta^{T}\circ T(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ TN^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ\mult^{T},

which we reduce to proving the following relations:

TN^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{TT})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{TT}))=\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}

\sigma^{T}\circ\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x}=T\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T})

For the first, we have the commutativity of the following diagram, omitting
horizontal 1-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mult^{T}\cdot\mathsf{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot T\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\alpha^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{T}\cdot\mathsf{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mult^{T}}

then we observe that

\displaystyle T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})

\displaystyle\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{TT})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{TT}))

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\theta^{T})\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{TT})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{TT}))

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})),

and finally recall from (
3.4
) that

\mult^{T}\circ(\sigma^{T}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ\nat^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}=T\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}),

so that we may calculate

\displaystyle T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})

\displaystyle\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{TT})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{TT}))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{(T\phi)^{*}}\circ\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{(T\phi)^{*}}\circ\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\sigma^{T}\cdot\mathsf{id})\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{(T\phi)^{*}}\circ\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=T\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ{\tilde{\mult}}^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\sigma^{T}\cdot\mathsf{id})\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{(T\phi)^{*}}\circ\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=T\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(T\nat^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ{\tilde{\mult}}^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=T\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ T{\tilde{\nat}}^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ{\tilde{\mult}}^{T}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T}))

\displaystyle=T\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ T{\tilde{\nat}}^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ T\hat{\alpha}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T}))

The second follows by applying the mate correspondence twice: we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{T}\circ\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x}\circ(\eta\cdot(\eta\circ 1))\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{T}\circ 1^{\lor}\circ\eta

\displaystyle=\sigma^{T}\circ\eta\circ 1

\displaystyle=T\eta\circ\unit^{T}\circ 1

\displaystyle=T(\eta\circ 1)\circ\unit^{T},

and

\displaystyle T\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(\eta\cdot(\eta\circ 1))\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=T\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ\mult^{T}\circ(T\eta\cdot T(\eta\circ 1))\circ(\unit^{T}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=T\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}\circ T(\eta\cdot(\eta\circ 1))\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T})\circ\rho^{-1}\circ\unit^{T}

\displaystyle=T(1^{\lor}\circ\eta)\circ\unit^{T}

\displaystyle=T(\eta\circ 1)\circ\unit^{T}.

We obtain (
5.15
) via the following calculation:

\displaystyle T(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})

\displaystyle\circ TN\phi^{*}_{a}\circ T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{TT})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{TT}))\circ(N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})^{-1}

\displaystyle=T(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ\mult^{T}\circ(\mult^{T}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T}))

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mu^{TF}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\sigma^{T}\cdot\sigma^{T}))

\displaystyle=\mult^{T}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T})\circ(\mu^{TF}\cdot\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x}).

Now, we apply (
5.10
), (
5.11
),
(
5.12
),
(c)
from Lemma

5.1
, (
5.13
), (
5.14
),
(
5.15
) in sucession, to obtain

\displaystyle(\mu^{F}

\displaystyle\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ((\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\cdot m_{Fa\cdot\phi^{*}})\circ(N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ((\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\cdot(m_{Fa}\cdot m^{\lor}_{\phi^{*}}))\circ(N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\cdot\theta^{TT})

\displaystyle=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ((\mu^{F}\cdot Fm_{a})\cdot(\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{Sx}\cdot m^{\lor}_{\phi^{*}}))\circ N^{\phi^{*}_{S}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\circ(N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\cdot\theta^{TT})

\displaystyle=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\mu^{FS})\cdot(\phi^{*}_{m}\cdot m^{\lor}_{\phi^{*}}))\circ(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\circ N^{\phi^{*}_{S}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}}_{a}\circ(N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\cdot\theta^{TT})

\displaystyle=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot FS\mu)\cdot(\phi^{*}_{m}\cdot m^{\lor}_{\phi^{*}}))\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a\cdot Sa}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mult^{TF}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\theta^{T}\cdot\theta^{TT}))\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mu^{TF}\cdot\mult^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{x}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot N^{(T\phi)^{*}}_{a})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\theta^{T}\cdot\theta^{TT}))\circ\alpha

\displaystyle=(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot TN^{\phi^{*}}_{a})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ\alpha

which confirms the associativity law.

We conclude the proof by confirming that
(Ff,F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f})
is
a horizontal lax
T
-algebra morphism, for any given horizontal lax

S
-algebra morphism
(f,\zeta)
: indeed, note that the following
diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{Fx}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{Fx}\cdot 1_{Fx}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{Fy}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{Fy}\cdot 1_{Fy}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fb\cdot\phi^{*}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{Ff}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{Ff}\cdot 1_{Ff}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon^{F}\cdot\unit^{\phi^{*}}_{y}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x})\cdot T(Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fb\cdot\phi^{*}_{y})\cdot T(Fb\cdot\phi^{*}_{y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x})\cdot(TFa\cdot(T\phi)^{*}_{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fb\cdot\phi^{*}_{y})\cdot(TFb\cdot(T\phi)^{*}_{y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fa\cdot FSa)\cdot(\phi^{*}_{Sx}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}_{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Fb\cdot FSb)\cdot(\phi^{*}_{Sy}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}_{y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fa\cdot\phi^{*}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fb\cdot\phi^{*}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f})\cdot T(F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\cdot\theta^{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f})\cdot(TF\zeta\cdot(T\phi)^{*}_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\phi^{*}}_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\phi^{*}}_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F\zeta\cdot FS\zeta)\cdot(\phi^{*}_{Sf}\cdot(T\phi)^{*}_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\zeta\cdot\phi^{*}_{f}}

via pasting of naturality and modification squares.

Functoriality is confirmed componentwise.
∎

We close this section with a comparative analysis of
Theorem
5.2
with the notions of change-of-base for internal

T
-categories described in
[
33
]
, and with the notions of
change-of-base for enriched
T
-categories described in
[
12
, Sections 5 and
6]
; we confirm all of these generalize to our setting. The description
of our main object of study, the functor
(\overline{T},\,\mathcal{V})\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}\to\mathsf{Cat}(T,\,\mathcal{V})
induced by

-\boldsymbol{\cdot}1\colon\mathcal{V}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}\to\Span(\mathcal{V})
, must be postponed to Section

8
.

5.1.
Internal
T
-categories:

Let
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{E}
be categories with pullbacks, with respective
cartesian monads
S
,
T
on
\mathcal{D}
,
\mathcal{E}
. We consider the
equipments
\mathbb{D}=\Span(\mathcal{D})
and
\mathbb{E}=\Span(\mathcal{E})
,
and, abusing notation, we denote by
S
and
T
the induced strong
monads on
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{E}
.

Using the terminology of
[
33
]
, we consider a cartesian monad oplax
morphism
(P,\phi)\colon S\to T
and a cartesian monad lax morphism
(Q,\psi)\colon T\to S
. Their underlying data is given by

–

pullback-preserving functors
P,Q\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
,

–

a cartesian natural transformation
\phi\colon PS\to TP
,

–

a natural transformation
\psi\colon SQ\to QT
.

We note
P
and
Q
induce strong functors
\hat{P}\colon\mathbb{D}\to\mathbb{E}
,
\hat{Q}\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
, and
\phi
,
\psi
induce vertical transformations
\hat{\phi}
,
\hat{\psi}
, which, in
turn, define a monad oplax morphism
(\hat{P},\hat{\phi})
and a monad lax
morphism
(\hat{Q},\hat{\psi})
.

We conclude, by Theorem
5.2
that
(\hat{Q},\hat{\psi})

defines a functor
\hat{Q}_{!}\colon\mathsf{Cat}(T,\,\mathcal{V})\to\mathsf{Cat}(S,\,\mathcal{V})
. Moreover, since
\phi
is cartesian,
\hat{\phi}
has a strong conjoint, and since
\hat{P}
is strong,
\hat{P}\hat{\phi}
also has a strong conjoint; therefore
we may also conclude that
(\hat{P},\hat{\phi})
induces a functor
\hat{P}_{!}\colon\mathsf{Cat}(S,\,\mathcal{V})\to\mathsf{Cat}(T,\,\mathcal{V})
.

In fact, this notion of change-of-base can easily be extended to include
Burroni’s
T
-categories
[
8
]
. This would require a notion of
horizontal lax
T
-algebra for
T
an
oplax
monad (possible with
merely a couple of adjustments), and a replacement of lax functors with oplax
functors in Theorem
5.2
. We leave a pursuit of these results
and possible applications for future work.

5.2.
Enriched
T
-categories:

Two instances of change-of-base are constructed in
[
12
]
; we begin by
fixing a distributive monoidal category
\mathcal{V}
, and let
\mathbb{D}=\mathcal{V}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}
. Therein, a
lax extension
of a
\mathsf{Set}
-monad
T
to
\mathcal{V}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}
is a normal lax monad on
\mathbb{D}
with underlying

\mathsf{Set}
-monad
T
.

First, we suppose we have two normal lax monads
S
and
T
on
\mathbb{D}
, and let
\phi\colon T\to S
be a vertical transformation,
so that
(\mathsf{id},\phi)\colon S\to T
defines a monad lax morphism. This is
precisely the data described in
[
12
, Section 5]
, restated in a double
categorical language. Theorem
5.2
produces a functor
(S,\,\mathcal{V})\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}\to(T,\,\mathcal{V})\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}
, which coincides with the
algebraic functor

construction in the aforementioned work.

Now, let
\mathcal{W}
be another distributive monoidal category, let
\mathbb{E}=\mathcal{W}\text{-}\mathsf{Mat}
, and let
F\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{W}
be a normal lax monoidal
functor, preserving the initial object.
F
induces a normal lax functor
\hat{F}\colon\mathbb{D}\to\mathbb{E}
with
\hat{F}_{0}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{Set}}
.

We let
T
and
S
be a lax monads on
\mathbb{D}
and on
\mathbb{E}
, respectively, with the same underlying monad on
\mathsf{Set}
; in other
words,
S
and
T
are
lax extensions
of the same
\mathsf{Set}
-monad.

Given a vertical transformation
\phi\colon T\hat{F}\to\hat{F}S
, such
that
\phi_{0}
is the identity and such that
(\hat{F},\phi)\colon S\to T
is a monad lax morphism, we may apply Theorem
5.2

to produce a functor
\hat{F}_{!}\colon(S,\,\mathcal{V})\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}\to(T,\,\mathcal{W})\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}
; this is
precisely the functor constructed in
[
12
, Section 6]
.

We should highlight that all normality conditions can be omitted, as well as
the preservation of the initial object by
F
, and still obtain
change-of-base functors. This normality-free setting for both instances of
change-of-base was already studied in
[
24
, Sections 3.4, 3.5]
, for
thin categories
\mathcal{V}
.

6.
Induced adjunction

As observed in
[
33
, Section 6.7]
, an adequate notion of adjunction
between cartesian monads will induce an adjunction on the categories of
internal
T
-categories, which has proven fruitful in their study.
Moreover, in
[
24
, Section 3]
, several change-of-base adjunctions
between categories of (monad, quantale)-categories are studied as well. Our
aim is to extend these ideas to arbitrary horizontal lax algebras, aiming to
compare the enriched and the internal notions of generalized multicategory.

Throughout this section, our setting is a conjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S,\mathbb{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T,\mathbb{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F,\,\phi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\,\psi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

in the double category
\mathsf{Mnd}(\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}})
, such that
\mathbb{D}
and

\mathbb{E}
are equipments, and
\phi
,
T\phi
have strong
conjoints. We denote the unit and counit by
\hat{\eta},\hat{\varepsilon}
, respectively.

We recall that

–

(F,\phi)\colon(S,\mathbb{D})\to(T,\mathbb{E})
is a monad oplax
morphism,

–

(G,\psi)\colon(T,\mathbb{E})\to(S,\mathbb{D})
is a monad lax
morphism,

–

we have an adjunction
F\dashv G
in
\mathsf{PsDbCat}_{\mathsf{lax}}
with unit
\hat{\eta}
and counit
\hat{\varepsilon}
,

–

and by doctrinal adjunction,
F
is strong, and
\phi
,
\psi

are mates,

so that by Theorem
5.2
, we may construct functors

F_{!}\colon\LaxSHAlg\to\LaxTHAlg\quad\text{and}\quad G_{!}\colon\LaxTHAlg\to\LaxSHAlg

induced by
(F,\phi^{*})
and
(G,\psi_{!})
, respectively.

Theorem 6.1
.

We have an adjunction
F_{!}\dashv G_{!}
.

Proof.

To fix notation, for
f\colon x\to Gy
and
g\colon Fx\to y
, we
let
f^{\sharp}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{y}\circ Ff
and
g^{\flat}=Gg\circ\hat{\eta}_{x}
. This is similarly defined for 2-cells.

We claim that the hom-isomorphism and its inverse are given by

(6.1)

(f,\zeta)\mapsto(f^{\sharp},\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}),\quad\text{and}\quad(g,\xi)\mapsto(g^{\flat},\xi^{\lor\flat\land})

where we use
(-)^{\lor}
and
(-)^{\land}
as short-hand for mates. To
be explicit, these are respectively given by

\displaystyle\zeta^{\lor}=\rho\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\circ\zeta\quad

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\xi^{\lor}=\xi\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\rho^{-1},

\displaystyle\theta^{\land}=(\theta\cdot(\nu\circ 1))\circ\rho^{-1}\quad

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\chi^{\land}=\rho\circ(\chi\cdot(1\circ\varepsilon))

for adequate 2-cells
\zeta,\theta
in
\mathbb{D}
and
\xi,\chi
are 2-cells in
\mathbb{E}
.

To make sure that the horizontal composition for these mates of 2-cells is
defined, note that

\hat{\varepsilon}_{Ty}\circ F\psi_{y}\circ FSf=T\hat{\varepsilon}_{y}\circ\phi_{Gy}\circ FSf=T\hat{\varepsilon}_{y}\circ TFf\circ\phi_{x}=Tf^{\sharp}\circ\phi_{x},

GTg\circ G\phi_{x}\circ\hat{\eta}_{Sx}=GTg\circ\psi_{Gx}\circ S\hat{\eta}_{x}=\psi_{y}\circ SFg\circ S\hat{\eta}_{x}=\psi_{y}\circ Sg^{\flat}.

Since
\lor
,
\land
and
\flat
,
\sharp
are pairs of
inverse operators, we find that the maps in (
6.1
) are
each others’ inverse.

To check these maps are natural, let
(k,\omega)\colon(w,d,\upsilon,\mu)\to(x,a,\upsilon,\mu)
be a horizontal lax
S
algebra morphism, and
(h,\chi)\colon(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)\to(z,c,\upsilon,\mu)
a horizontal
lax
T
-algebra morphism. We have

\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ(F\omega\cdot\phi^{*}_{k})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\circ\rho^{-1}=\zeta^{\lor\sharp}\circ F\omega=(\zeta\circ\omega)^{\lor\sharp},

((G\chi\cdot\psi_{!h})\circ\zeta)^{\lor}=G\chi\circ\zeta^{\lor},\quad(G\chi\circ\zeta^{\lor})^{\sharp}=\hat{\varepsilon}_{c}\circ FG\chi\circ F\zeta^{\lor}=\chi\circ\zeta^{\lor\sharp}

and reach our desired conclusion via mate correspondence.

So we’re left with proving that
(f^{\sharp},\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land})

and
(g^{\flat},\xi^{\lor\flat\land})
are horizontal lax algebra
morphisms. Since the proofs are similar, we omit the second one.

We must check the following identities hold:

\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ F_{!}\upsilon=\upsilon\circ 1_{f^{\sharp}}\quad\text{and}\quad\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ F_{!}\mu=\mu\circ(\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\cdot T\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land})

Since
(f,\zeta)
is a lax
S
-algebra morphism, we have

\zeta\circ\upsilon=G_{!}\upsilon\circ 1_{f},

so that

\displaystyle\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ F_{!}\upsilon

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\lor\sharp}\circ F\upsilon\circ\unit^{F}

\displaystyle=(\zeta\circ\upsilon)^{\lor\sharp}\circ\unit^{F}

\displaystyle=\hat{\varepsilon}_{b}\circ FG\upsilon\circ F\unit^{G}\circ F1_{f}\circ\unit^{F}

\displaystyle=\upsilon\circ\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\circ\unit^{FG}\circ 1_{Ff}

\displaystyle=\upsilon\circ 1_{f^{\sharp}},

which gives the unit law for
(f^{\sharp},\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land})
.

For the multiplication law, we will confirm that

\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})=\mu\circ(\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\cdot T\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\hat{\alpha}

via mate correspondence.

On one hand, we have

\displaystyle\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ(\mu^{F}\cdot\mult^{\phi^{*}}_{x})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\eta\cdot(\eta\circ 1)))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\circ(\mu^{F}\cdot(\eta\circ 1))\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\lor\sharp}\circ\mu^{F}

\displaystyle=(\zeta\circ\mu)^{\lor\sharp}\circ\mult^{F},

while on the other, we begin by noting that

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}

\displaystyle\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\eta\cdot(\eta\circ 1)))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot(\phi_{a}\cdot(\eta\circ 1)))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\gamma^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot(\phi_{a}\cdot(\eta\circ 1)))\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot\phi_{a})\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\eta))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot\phi_{a})\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\unit^{T}))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot\phi_{a})\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{id}\cdot T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\rho^{-1})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot\phi_{a})\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id}),

hence, if we write

Y=(\mathsf{id}\cdot\mult^{T})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\sigma^{T}))\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{\phi^{*}}_{a}\circ\hat{\alpha},\quad Z=(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\eta\cdot(\eta\circ 1)))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho^{-1})\circ\rho^{-1}\\

we deduce that

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle\circ(\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\cdot T\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land})\circ Y\circ Z

\displaystyle=\mu\circ(\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land}\cdot T\zeta^{\lor\sharp\land})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T(\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot T\rho^{-1})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\eta)\cdot\phi_{a}))\circ(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{id})

\displaystyle=\mu\circ\big(\zeta^{\lor\sharp}\cdot(T\zeta^{\lor\sharp}\circ\phi_{a})\big)

\displaystyle=\mu\circ\big((\varepsilon_{b}\circ F\zeta^{\lor})\cdot(T\varepsilon_{b}\circ TF\zeta^{\lor}\circ\phi_{a})\big)

\displaystyle=\mu\circ\big((\varepsilon_{b}\circ F\zeta^{\lor})\cdot(T\varepsilon_{b}\circ\phi_{Gb}\circ FS\zeta^{\lor})\big)

\displaystyle=\mu\circ\big((\varepsilon_{b}\circ F\zeta^{\lor})\cdot(\varepsilon_{Tb}\circ F\psi_{b}\circ FS\zeta^{\lor})\big)

\displaystyle=\mu\circ(\varepsilon_{b}\cdot\varepsilon_{Tb})\circ\big(F\zeta^{\lor}\cdot(F\psi_{b}\circ FS\zeta^{\lor})\big)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{b}\circ F\mu^{G}\circ\mult^{F}\circ\big(F\zeta^{\lor}\cdot(F\psi_{b}\circ FS\zeta^{\lor})\big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\mu^{G}\circ\big(\zeta^{\lor}\cdot(\psi_{b}\circ S\zeta^{\lor})\big)\Big)^{\sharp}\circ\mult^{F},

so we conclude it is sufficient to show that

(\zeta\circ\mu)^{\lor}=\mu^{G}\circ\big(\zeta^{\lor}\cdot(\psi_{b}\circ S\zeta^{\lor})),

and indeed, we have

\displaystyle(\zeta\circ\mu)^{\lor}

\displaystyle=\rho\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\circ G_{!}\mu\circ(\zeta\cdot S\zeta)

\displaystyle=\rho\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\circ(\mu^{G}\cdot\mult^{\psi_{!}}_{b})\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ{\tilde{\nat}}^{\psi_{!}}_{b}\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\chi^{S})\circ(\zeta\cdot S\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mu^{G}\circ\rho\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ\hat{\alpha}^{-1}\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\gamma\cdot\mathsf{id}))\circ\hat{\alpha}\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\cdot(\psi_{b}\cdot(1\circ\delta)))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\chi^{S})\circ(\zeta\cdot S\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mu^{G}\circ(\rho\cdot\rho)\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\cdot(\psi_{b}\cdot(1\circ\delta)))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\chi^{S})\circ(\zeta\cdot S\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mu^{G}\circ(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\cdot\psi_{b})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\rho)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta))\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot\chi^{S})\circ(\zeta\cdot S\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mu^{G}\circ(\rho\cdot\mathsf{id})\circ((\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta)\cdot\psi_{b})\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot S\rho)\circ(\mathsf{id}\cdot S(\mathsf{id}\cdot\delta))\circ(\zeta\cdot S\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mu^{G}\circ\big(\zeta^{\lor}\cdot(\psi_{b}\circ S\zeta^{\lor})\big),

which concludes the proof.
∎

For the purposes of applying this adjunction to the study of full faithfulness
of
F_{!}
and subsequent applications to descent theory, it is useful to
establish criteria for the invertibility of the unit and counit of the
adjunction
F_{!}\dashv G_{!}
, which are provided by the following results:

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)
be a horizontal lax
T
-algebra. If
\hat{\varepsilon}_{y}
and
F\psi_{y}
are invertible, then
\hat{\varepsilon}_{(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)}
is invertible if and only if
\nat^{\hat{\varepsilon}^{*}}_{b}
is invertible.

Proof.

We have
\hat{\varepsilon}_{(y,b,\upsilon,\mu)}=(\hat{\varepsilon}_{y},\mathsf{id}^{\lor\sharp\land})
. We first observe that, up to coherence isomorphisms, we have
\mathsf{id}^{\lor\sharp\land}=\Omega
, where

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{67.93365pt}{-53.24307pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
